Sick Sinus Syndrome 2, Autosomal Dominant

Mendelian MONDO:0008102 Pathograph 29 Show in embeddings browser Familial Sick Sinus Syndrome Channelopathy

Sick sinus syndrome 2 (SSS2) is the autosomal dominant, HCN4-related form of familial sinus node dysfunction. HCN4 encodes the pore-forming subunit of the hyperpolarization-activated, cyclic-nucleotide-gated pacemaker channel that carries the funny current (I_f), the inward current responsible for the early phase of diastolic depolarization in sinoatrial node pacemaker cells and for the cAMP-dependent autonomic modulation of heart rate. Heterozygous loss-of-function variants act either by shifting the voltage dependence of activation to more negative potentials, by dominant-negative suppression of wild-type subunits, by defective channel synthesis and trafficking, or by truncating the cyclic-nucleotide-binding domain so that the channel becomes cAMP-insensitive. The result is a smaller inward diastolic current, a slower rate of diastolic depolarization, and depressed sinoatrial automaticity — clinically, sinus bradycardia, sinus pauses, chronotropic incompetence, syncope, and a high burden of atrial fibrillation. A distinctive feature of the HCN4 form, not shared with the sodium-channel forms of familial sick sinus syndrome, is co-segregation of left ventricular noncompaction cardiomyopathy (and, in some pedigrees, dilation of the ascending aorta) with the bradycardia, indicating that the same channel lesion also perturbs myocardial development.

Inheritance

1
Autosomal dominant HP:0000006
SSS2 segregates as an autosomal dominant trait. All reported disease variants are heterozygous, and the dominant mechanism is usually dominant-negative suppression of wild-type HCN4 subunits within the heterotetrameric channel rather than simple haploinsufficiency. Penetrance and expressivity vary widely, from asymptomatic sinus bradycardia detected only on screening to syncope requiring pacemaker implantation.
Autosomal dominant inheritance
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:17646576 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Electrophysiological testing performed on 2 affected family members confirmed significant isolated sinus node dysfunction. Segregation analysis suggested autosomal-dominant inheritance."
Formal segregation analysis in a 16-member HCN4-G480R family established autosomal dominant transmission of isolated sinus node dysfunction.
PMID:12750403 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Coexpression experiments showed a dominant-negative effect of HCN4-573X subunits on wild-type subunits."
Co-expression of mutant with wild-type subunits demonstrates the dominant-negative mechanism underlying dominant transmission.

Mechanistic Hypotheses

1
Sinoatrial Pacemaker Automaticity Failure Model (HCN4 specialization)
sinoatrial_automaticity_failure_model CANONICAL
Evidence balance 2 support
The disease-specific specialization of the module-level cardiac_ion_channel_repolarization group of the same name. In SSS2 the depressed current is specifically the hyperpolarization-activated funny current I_f carried by HCN4, so the bradyarrhythmia arises from a slowed diastolic depolarization slope in sinoatrial pacemaker cells rather than from depressed sodium-dependent conduction, and syncope follows from hypoperfusion during pauses rather than from ventricular tachyarrhythmia.
Pathograph links
HCN4 Loss-of-Function Variant -> Reduced Funny Current and Slowed Diastolic Depolarization DIRECT Reduced channel availability, a hyperpolarizing shift of the activation curve, or dominant-negative suppression of wild-type subunits lowers the inward diastolic current carried by I_f.
Reduced Funny Current and Slowed Diastolic Depolarization -> Sinoatrial Node Automaticity Failure DIRECT A slower diastolic depolarization slope lengthens the pacemaker cycle and depresses automaticity of the node as a whole.
Sinoatrial Node Automaticity Failure -> Syncope and Sudden Cardiac Death INDIRECT KNOWN INTERMEDIATES Severe bradycardia or a prolonged pause reduces cerebral perfusion, producing presyncope and syncope. Intermediates: prolonged sinus pauses and asystole; cerebral hypoperfusion

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

5
By what route does a loss-of-function variant in the sinoatrial pacemaker channel produce dilatation of the ascending aorta?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN q_sss2_aortic_wall_mechanism
Attached to
phenotypes#Dilatation of the Ascending Aorta pathophysiology#Impaired Ventricular Trabecular Compaction
Aortic root and ascending aortic dilatation has now been reported in several independent HCN4 families and in a majority of carriers in the largest series, so the association is replicated. What is missing is any mechanism. HCN4 has no described role in the aortic wall, no HCN4 animal model has been reported with an aortic phenotype, and neither of the two candidate routes has been tested: a direct requirement for HCN4 in neural-crest-derived or second-heart-field-derived outflow tract mesenchyme, or a haemodynamic consequence of lifelong bradycardia acting on the wall through increased stroke volume. The distinction is not academic, because it decides whether pacing a carrier would be expected to slow the aortic course. Until it is resolved, imaging surveillance in carriers rests on the observed association alone.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35257104 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Earliest expression of HCN4 has been reported to be a potential marker for the first heart field."
The one developmental fact that makes a direct route plausible: HCN4 marks an early cardiac progenitor population, so a developmental role in outflow structures is not excluded. It is a marker claim rather than a functional one, which is exactly the gap.
Is the ventricular noncompaction of SSS2 a developmental consequence of HCN4 loss in the early myocardium, or a secondary remodelling response to lifelong bradycardia?
OPEN QUESTION OPEN q_sss2_noncompaction_developmental_or_rate_driven
Attached to
pathophysiology#Impaired Ventricular Trabecular Compaction phenotypes#Left Ventricular Noncompaction
Both readings fit the human data. The developmental reading is supported by HCN4 expression in early cardiac progenitors, by noncompaction being present already in fetal and adolescent carriers, and by Hcn4 sitting downstream of a transcriptional cascade rather than being an isolated channel gene: in mouse, loss of Shox2 abolishes Tbx3 and Hcn4 expression and leaves a hypoplastic sinoatrial node. That cascade is characterised for the sinoatrial node and not for ventricular trabeculation, so it shows that HCN4 has a developmental context without showing that it has one in the compacting ventricle. The haemodynamic reading is supported by the observation that HCN4 carriers with noncompaction have preserved ejection fraction and mild ventricular dilatation, a picture that could equally reflect volume loading at a low heart rate. One experiment on the same gene bears on the haemodynamic reading, though only on part of it: a knock-in mouse with a gain-of-function HCN4 allele runs chronically faster and develops no structural cardiomyopathy over twelve months. That speaks to adult remodelling under a sustained rate change, in the opposite direction from the bradycardia at issue and in a species whose baseline rate is an order of magnitude higher; it says nothing about whether lifelong bradycardia perturbs trabecular compaction during development, which is the reading actually in dispute. Settling that needs a loss-of-function HCN4 model examined for trabecular architecture, and a comparison of noncompaction burden between paced and unpaced carriers.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:42358349 SUPPORT INDIRECT Model Organism
"Histological analysis did not reveal structural changes consistent with tachycardia-induced cardiomyopathy. Cardiac morphology, fibrosis, and contractility were indistinguishable between genotypes up to 12 months of age."
A sustained HCN4-driven change in heart rate produced no structural cardiomyopathy in the adult mouse heart. It bears on the adult-remodelling reading only, and in the opposite direction from the bradycardia at issue, so it constrains the question rather than answering it.
PMID:19166829 SUPPORT INDIRECT Model Organism
"Genetically, the lack of Tbx3 and Hcn4 expression, along with ectopic activation of Nppa, Cx40, and Nkx2-5 in the Shox2(-/-) SAN region, indicates a failure in SAN differentiation."
Places Hcn4 inside a developmental transcriptional programme rather than treating it as a channel gene expressed only in the mature node, which is what makes a developmental reading of the structural phenotype coherent at all. Two inference steps separate it from the question: it is mouse, and the programme it describes builds the sinoatrial node, not the compacted ventricular wall.
PMID:34088380 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"Ion channel gene mutations should be searched systematically in patients with LVNC associated with either bradycardia or biventricular noncompaction, particularly when LV systolic function is preserved."
The preserved-function, biventricular pattern is the observation both readings have to explain, and it is compatible with either a developmental or a volume-loading origin, which is why the question stays open.
What functional evidence should be required before an HCN4 variant found in a bradycardic patient is called causal for SSS2?
CONTROVERSY OPEN q_sss2_variant_causality_threshold
Attached to
genetic#HCN4 loss-of-function variants pathophysiology#HCN4 Loss-of-Function Variant
SSS2 is defined by an HCN4 variant, so the entry's causal claim is only as good as the variant calls behind it. HCN4 variant discovery has outrun functional characterisation: most reported variants have never been patch-clamped, and at least one variant that had been treated as pathogenic, and linked in the literature to sudden infant death and malignant epilepsy, was found on characterisation to be indistinguishable from wild type in voltage dependence, kinetics, cAMP-gating and surface expression, and not to segregate with disease in the proband's own family. The alleles curated in this entry all carry electrophysiological or segregation evidence, which is the standard this discussion argues for, but the boundary is not settled and the field has no agreed threshold. Practically, this bears on cascade screening: a carrier identified on a panel without functional or segregation support has not been shown to have SSS2.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:33095298 SUPPORT In Vitro
"However, detailed electrophysiological and cell biological characterization of HCN4 V759I in Xenopus laevis oocytes and embryonic rat cardiomyocytes, respectively, did not reveal any obvious abnormality."
A variant treated as pathogenic behaves as wild type on functional testing, which is the concrete case the controversy rests on.
PMID:33095298 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In good agreement, the clinically likewise affected mother of the patient does not exhibit the reported HCN4 variance."
Adds the segregation half of the argument: the affected relative does not carry the variant, so the family data contradict causality independently of the electrophysiology.
Should an HCN4 carrier with sinus node dysfunction but no documented atrial fibrillation receive anticoagulation?
KNOWLEDGE GAP OPEN q_sss2_anticoagulation_without_documented_af
Attached to
treatments#Stroke Prevention in Atrial Fibrillation phenotypes#Atrial Fibrillation
Two independent lines of evidence say sinus node dysfunction raises ischaemic stroke risk whether or not atrial fibrillation has been documented, which has been read as an atrial myopathy underlying both. If that is right, the risk-score logic that gates anticoagulation on a documented arrhythmia is the wrong gate. Nothing resolves this for SSS2 in particular: no cohort in either source was genotyped, no trial has tested anticoagulation in sinus node dysfunction without atrial fibrillation, and HCN4 carriers are a young population in whom the bleeding side of the trade-off runs for decades. The practical question is whether prolonged rhythm monitoring in carriers, which they often already have through an implanted device or loop recorder, converts the question into an ordinary documented-atrial-fibrillation decision often enough to make the untested group small.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40315941 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"SND is an independent risk marker for the development of IS in individuals without AF."
States the finding the gap turns on, that the excess stroke risk does not require documented atrial fibrillation. The cohort is unselected sinus node dysfunction rather than HCN4 carriers.
PMID:37977871 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Future studies are needed to investigate whether interventions targeting stroke prevention, such as anticoagulation therapy, can help to prevent stroke in patients with SND."
The authors name the same gap and state that it is untested, which is why this is recorded as an open question rather than as a treatment recommendation.
Does the mouse carrying the human cAMP-insensitive HCN4-573X allele reproduce human chronotropic incompetence, given that its relative rate adaptation to exercise is preserved?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH OPEN q_sss2_chronotropic_incompetence_model_mismatch
Attached to
pathophysiology#Loss of cAMP-Dependent Rate Modulation phenotypes#Chronotropic Incompetence
The hHCN4-573X mouse carries the human disease allele and loses cAMP sensitivity of I_f completely, yet the relative extent of its heart rate regulation during physical activity is preserved: basal and maximal rates fall, but the animal still scales its rate with demand. The human index patient was described with chronotropic incompetence. Both can be true if human chronotropic incompetence is a consequence of the depressed maximal rate rather than of a lost ability to modulate, and this entry's pathophysiology is written that way. The mismatch is nonetheless real and unresolved: it is not established that cAMP insensitivity is what produces the clinical exercise limitation, and the mouse is the only experiment that separates the two. Deciding it needs formal chronotropic-index testing in genotyped carriers of cyclic-nucleotide-binding-domain truncations, compared against carriers of pore and trafficking alleles that retain cAMP sensitivity.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:19570998 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Despite the complete loss of cAMP sensitivity of I(f), the relative extent of SAN cell frequency and heart rate regulation are preserved."
States the mismatch directly: the model loses the molecular property proposed to underlie chronotropic incompetence without losing rate regulation.
⚙

Pathophysiology

6
HCN4 Loss-of-Function Variant
A heterozygous loss-of-function variant in HCN4 alters the pore-forming subunit of the cardiac pacemaker channel. Reported disease alleles cluster in three structural regions with three corresponding biophysical consequences: pore-domain missense changes (G480R, G482R) that impair channel synthesis, trafficking, and gating; C-linker and cyclic-nucleotide-binding-domain changes (S672R, K530N) that shift activation gating; and C-terminal truncations (573X, 695X) that delete the cyclic-nucleotide-binding domain. All are heterozygous, and most act dominant-negatively on wild-type subunits within the heterotetramer.
Sinoatrial Pacemaker Automaticity Failure Model (HCN4 specialization)

PMID:16407510 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We found that sinus bradycardia in members of a large family was associated with a mutation in the gene coding for the pacemaker HCN4 ion channel."
The founding family study links a heterozygous HCN4 variant to inherited sinus bradycardia.
PMID:17646576 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Synthesis and expression of the wild-type and mutant HCN4 channel on the plasma membrane tested in human embryonic kidney 293 cells using biotinylation and Western blot analysis demonstrated a reduction in synthesis and a trafficking defect in mutant compared with wild-type channels."
Documents the synthesis/trafficking route to reduced channel availability for the pore-domain class of HCN4 alleles.
PMID:26835093 SUPPORT Other
"All mutations described so far are loss-of-function and in agreement with the role of funny channels, the predominant type of arrhythmia found is bradycardia."
Confirms that the HCN4 disease alleles reported to date are uniformly loss-of-function.
Reduced Funny Current and Slowed Diastolic Depolarization
The funny current I_f is the hyperpolarization-activated inward cation current that drives the early phase of spontaneous diastolic depolarization in sinoatrial node pacemaker cells. Mutant HCN4 channels activate at more negative voltages than wild-type channels, so at the diastolic potentials actually reached by a pacemaker cell less inward current flows. The diastolic depolarization slope falls, the interval to threshold lengthens, and the intrinsic firing rate of the node drops. The biophysical change has been described as mimicking mild vagal stimulation — a permanent, genetically imposed version of a physiological brake.
Sinoatrial Pacemaker Automaticity Failure Model (HCN4 specialization)

PMID:12750403 SUPPORT Other
"The cardiac pacemaker current I(f) is a major determinant of diastolic depolarization in sinus nodal cells and has a key role in heartbeat generation."
Establishes the normal role of I_f in diastolic depolarization that is lost in SSS2.
PMID:16407510 SUPPORT In Vitro
"These changes, which mimic those of mild vagal stimulation, slow the heart rate by decreasing the inward diastolic current."
States the biophysical mechanism directly: less inward diastolic current slows the heart rate.
PMID:23178648 SUPPORT In Vitro
"In contrast, heteromeric channels composed of mutant and wild-type subunits displayed a significant hyperpolarizing shift in the half-maximal activation voltage."
Shows that the hyperpolarizing gating shift emerges specifically in heteromeric mutant/wild-type channels, the situation in a heterozygous carrier.
Loss of cAMP-Dependent Rate Modulation
Beta-adrenergic stimulation raises intracellular cAMP, which binds the cyclic-nucleotide-binding domain of HCN4 and shifts activation to more positive voltages, accelerating diastolic depolarization; vagal tone does the reverse. Truncating HCN4 alleles such as 573X delete this domain entirely, so the residual current is insensitive to cAMP. This is the proposed mechanistic basis of chronotropic incompetence in SSS2 — a failure of rate adaptation that is separable from the resting bradycardia. The corresponding mouse model shows that cAMP sensitivity of I_f sets basal and maximal heart rate while relative rate regulation during exercise is partially preserved by other mechanisms.

Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:12750403 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Patch-clamp experiments showed that HCN4-573X channels mediated I(f)-like currents that were insensitive to increased cellular cAMP levels."
Direct demonstration that a truncating HCN4 allele abolishes cAMP responsiveness of the pacemaker current.
PMID:12750403 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Taken together, the clinical, genetic, and in vitro data provide a likely explanation for the patient's sinus bradycardia and the chronotropic incompetence."
Links the cAMP-insensitive channel phenotype to the patient's chronotropic incompetence.
PMID:19570998 SUPPORT INDIRECT Model Organism
"Our data demonstrate that cAMP-mediated regulation of I(f) determines basal and maximal heart rates but does not play an indispensable role in heart rate adaptation during physical activity."
A mouse model of the human 573X allele shows cAMP sensitivity of I_f sets basal and maximal rate. The same study finds that relative exercise rate adaptation is preserved, so the chronotropic claim rests on the loss of maximal rate rather than on a loss of rate adaptation itself.
Sinoatrial Node Automaticity Failure
Depressed pacemaker-cell firing translates into failure of the sinoatrial node to generate and deliver impulses at an appropriate rate: sinus bradycardia at rest, intermittent sinus pauses or arrest, and an inadequate rate response to demand. Because the node also anchors atrial activation, chronic bradycardia and the associated atrial electrical and structural remodeling raise susceptibility to atrial fibrillation, giving the tachycardia-bradycardia pattern that is unusually common in HCN4 carriers.

Animal models
Inducible cardiac-specific HCN4 knockout mouse (ciHCN4-KO) Mouse partially recapitulates Ablation reduces I_f and the spontaneous rate of isolated sinoatrial node myocytes and produces severe bradycardia in vivo, confirming that loss of HCN4 function is sufficient to depress nodal automaticity.

Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:17646576 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Electrophysiological testing performed on 2 affected family members confirmed significant isolated sinus node dysfunction."
Invasive electrophysiology in HCN4-G480R carriers confirms isolated sinoatrial node dysfunction as the tissue-level lesion.
PMID:16407510 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Thus, diminished function of pacemaker channels is linked to familial bradycardia."
States the causal chain from diminished pacemaker-channel function to the familial bradycardia phenotype.
Impaired Ventricular Trabecular Compaction
HCN4 is expressed during cardiac development, and in several independent pedigrees the same segregating HCN4 allele produces both bradycardia and left ventricular noncompaction — a myocardium with a persistently trabeculated, poorly compacted layer and deep intertrabecular recesses. This developmental arm is what distinguishes the HCN4 form of familial sick sinus syndrome from the sodium-channel forms, in which no structural abnormality is found. It is curated here as a parallel branch of the same genetic lesion rather than as maladaptive remodeling secondary to the arrhythmia: the noncompaction is present from childhood and does not follow a preceding phase of cardiomyocyte injury and neurohormonal activation. Some pedigrees additionally show dilation of the ascending aorta.

Left Ventricular Noncompaction Failure of trabecular compaction gives the two-layered noncompacted myocardium seen on echocardiography and cardiac MRI.

PMID:25145517 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"One of these, p.Gly482Arg in HCN4, segregated with the combined bradycardia and LVNC phenotype in the entire family."
Establishes co-segregation of a single HCN4 allele with both the electrical and the structural phenotype.
PMID:25145517 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Although mutations in HCN4 have been previously linked to bradycardia, our study provides the first evidence to our knowledge that mutations in this ion channel gene also may be associated with structural abnormalities of the myocardium."
States explicitly that HCN4 variants can produce a structural myocardial abnormality, not only an electrical one.
PMID:25145518 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The symptom complex of SND and NCCM is associated with heritable HCN4 defects."
An independent German cohort reaches the same conclusion for the combined sinus-node/noncompaction phenotype.
Syncope and Sudden Cardiac Death
Severe bradycardia or a prolonged sinus pause transiently abolishes effective cardiac output, producing presyncope and syncope from cerebral hypoperfusion; an unterminated pause carries a risk of asystolic sudden death. This is the shared clinical endpoint with the other inherited arrhythmia syndromes, reached in SSS2 through bradyarrhythmia rather than ventricular tachyarrhythmia. Prognosis is variable and allele-dependent: the G480R pore-domain family remained asymptomatic with normal exercise capacity throughout long-term follow-up and required no pacemaker.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34383451 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"About 50% of patients present with cerebral hypoperfusion (e.g., syncope, presyncope, lightheadedness, cerebrovascular accident)."
Supports cerebral hypoperfusion (syncope/presyncope) as the clinical endpoint of symptomatic sinus node dysfunction.
PMID:17646576 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Despite its critical location, this mutation carries a favorable prognosis without the need for pacemaker implantation during long-term follow-up."
Qualifies the outcome node: at least one HCN4 pore-domain allele carries a benign long-term course, so the outcome is allele- and family-dependent.

Phenotypes

Cardiovascular 5
Prolonged QT Interval VERY_RARE HP:0001657 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical feature is this phenotype This clinical feature is Prolonged QT interval (HP:0001657). HP:0001657 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:15123648 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A missense mutation, D553N, was found in a patient with sinus node dysfunction who showed recurrent syncope, QT prolongation in electrocardiogram, and polymorphic ventricular tachycardia, torsade de pointes."
Records QT prolongation, with torsade de pointes, in a genotyped HCN4 carrier.
PMID:39698436 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Electrocardiography (ECG) after birth revealed sinus bradycardia with a heart rate of 70–80 bpm, associated with a slightly prolonged QTc of 500."
An independent HCN4 carrier with QT prolongation. The value normalised on follow-up, which is part of why the phenotype is banded VERY_RARE and described as unsettled rather than asserted as a core feature.
Atrial Fibrillation FREQUENT HP:0005110 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical feature is this phenotype This clinical feature is Atrial fibrillation (HP:0005110). HP:0005110 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:28104484 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Moreover, HCN4 mutation carriers were more frequently associated with AF (43.8%) and LVNC (50%) and with older age at pacemaker implantation"
Quantifies atrial fibrillation at 43.8% of HCN4 mutation carriers, supporting the FREQUENT band (30-79%).
PMID:23178648 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The index patient developed tachycardia-bradycardia syndrome and persistent atrial fibrillation (AF) in an age-dependent fashion. Pedigree analysis identified eight affected family members with a similar course of disease."
An HCN4 pedigree in which tachycardia-bradycardia syndrome with persistent atrial fibrillation is the segregating phenotype.
PMID:24607718 SUPPORT INDIRECT In Vitro
"Our findings are consistent with HCN4 haploinsufficiency as the likely mechanism for early-onset AF in the p.Pro257Ser carrier."
Adds a second route to the atrial fibrillation arm. This carrier's allele fails to reach the membrane and does not co-localise with wild-type channel, so the atrial phenotype here follows from simple haploinsufficiency rather than from the dominant-negative suppression that predominates elsewhere in SSS2. The carrier was ascertained from an early-onset atrial fibrillation cohort rather than a sinus node dysfunction one, so reading the finding into SSS2 takes an inference step.

"more recently, a more complex phenotype has emerged, including primarily noncompaction cardiomyopathy (NCC), aortic dilatation but also mitral valve defects, early-onset atrial fibrillation, atrioventricular block, and ventricular tachycardia"
Places mitral valve disease in the extended HCN4 phenotype spectrum alongside the noncompaction and aortic findings already curated here.
PMID:35257104 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"a mildly dysplastic aortic valve with mild central regurgitation and no stenosis and mild mitral valve prolapse"
Documents mitral valve prolapse directly in a p.Gly482Arg carrier, giving an individual observation behind the spectrum statement above. The frequency is banded OCCASIONAL because it is reported in individual carriers rather than counted in any cohort.

"About 50% of patients present with cerebral hypoperfusion (e.g., syncope, presyncope, lightheadedness, cerebrovascular accident)."
Syncope is a direct manifestation of the cerebral hypoperfusion caused by sinus node dysfunction.
Atrioventricular Block HP:0001678 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical feature is this phenotype This clinical feature is Atrioventricular block (HP:0001678). HP:0001678 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26835093 SUPPORT Other
"Funny channel-linked arrhythmias, however, also include atrioventricular (AV) block and atrial fibrillation, in agreement with an emerging new concept according to which defective funny channels have a still unexplored role in impairing AV conduction and triggering atrial fibrillation."
A review places AV block in the HCN4 arrhythmia spectrum. The review itself describes the AV-conduction role as an emerging and still unexplored concept, so this is the weakest arm of the phenotype.

Chronotropic Incompetence HP:0033992 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical feature is this phenotype This clinical feature is Chronotropic incompetence (HP:0033992). HP:0033992 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:12750403 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Taken together, the clinical, genetic, and in vitro data provide a likely explanation for the patient's sinus bradycardia and the chronotropic incompetence."
Documents chronotropic incompetence in the index HCN4-573X patient and attributes it to the cAMP-insensitive pacemaker channel.
PMID:17646576 REFUTE Human Clinical
"All affected family members were asymptomatic with normal exercise capacity during long-term follow-up."
Counter-evidence that chronotropic incompetence is not obligate in SSS2: the HCN4-G480R family retained normal exercise capacity.
PMID:28465117 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Under maximum exercise test carriers achieved significantly lower heart rates than non-carrier family members, and percent heart rate reserve and percent corrected heart rate reserve were significantly lower in carriers."
A quantified heart-rate-reserve comparison between genotyped carriers and non-carrier relatives in the same family, which is the comparison the two case reports above cannot make.

Left Ventricular Noncompaction FREQUENT HP:0030682 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical feature is this phenotype This clinical feature is Left ventricular noncompaction (HP:0030682). HP:0030682 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.

PMID:28104484 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Moreover, HCN4 mutation carriers were more frequently associated with AF (43.8%) and LVNC (50%) and with older age at pacemaker implantation"
Quantifies left ventricular noncompaction at 50% of HCN4 mutation carriers, supporting the FREQUENT band (30-79%).
PMID:25145517 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Subsequent screening of HCN4 in 3 additional families with the same clinical combination of bradycardia and LVNC identified HCN4 mutations in each."
Replication across three additional families establishes noncompaction as part of the HCN4 phenotype rather than a chance co-occurrence.
Biventricular Noncompaction with Preserved Systolic Function OCCASIONAL Biventricular noncompaction cardiomyopathy HP:0012818 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical feature is this phenotype This clinical feature is Biventricular noncompaction cardiomyopathy (HP:0012818). HP:0012818 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34088380 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"we found that those patients had higher median LV ejection fraction (60% vs 40%; P < .001) and more biventricular noncompaction (53.1% vs 18.5%; P < .001) than the 81 other patients with LVNC"
Quantifies the preserved ejection fraction and the biventricular distribution. The 53.1% is the share among ion-channel-mutation noncompaction patients, that is, conditioned on already having noncompaction, not the share among SSS2 carriers. Since noncompaction itself is banded FREQUENT at 50% of HCN4 carriers, the implied share of all carriers is roughly a quarter, which is why this phenotype is banded OCCASIONAL.
PMID:34088380 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"both the 19 patients with an HCN4 mutation and the 11 patients with an RYR2 mutation presented with a higher LV ejection fraction and more frequent biventricular noncompaction than the 81 patients with LVNC but with no mutation in the ion channel gene, but only patients with HCN4 mutation..."
Confirms the direction of the effect in the 19 HCN4 carriers taken on their own, and adds the bradycardia that separates HCN4 from RYR2 within the ion-channel group. It gives a direction rather than an HCN4-specific percentage, so the band above is not derived from it.
Dilatation of the Ascending Aorta Ascending tubular aorta aneurysm HP:0004970 Human Phenotype Ontology (HP) Relation: this clinical feature is this phenotype This clinical feature is Dilatation of the ascending aorta, annotated with Ascending tubular aorta aneurysm (HP:0004970). HP:0004970 is a phenotype from the Human Phenotype Ontology.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35328031 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The HCN4 molecular variants influence the presence of a complex LVNC phenotype, sinus bradycardia and dilation of the ascending aorta."
Reports ascending aortic dilation in HCN4 families. The HPO binding is exact rather than approximate — "Dilatation of ascending aorta" is an EXACT synonym of HP:0004970, and the source measured the ascending aorta in the parasternal long-axis view indexed to body surface area, the same segment the term names. The series is small (six patients, four families) and the mechanism linking the pacemaker channel to the aortic wall is unknown, so the association is better established than its explanation.

Genetic Associations

1
HCN4 loss-of-function variants (Causative)
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this disease-associated gene is this gene This disease-associated gene is HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee. relationship_type: CAUSATIVE
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:26835093 SUPPORT Other
"All mutations described so far are loss-of-function and in agreement with the role of funny channels, the predominant type of arrhythmia found is bradycardia."
Confirms the uniform loss-of-function direction of HCN4 disease alleles, which holds for every allele curated here except P883R.
PMID:31481236 REFUTE In Vitro
"Patch-clamp recordings of HCN4-P883R channels expressed in HEK293 cells showed remarkable alterations of channel properties shifting the half-maximal activation voltage to more depolarized potentials, while channel deactivation was faster compared to wild-type (WT)."
The counterexample to the uniform loss-of-function statement above. A depolarising shift of the activation curve increases channel availability at diastolic potentials, the opposite of the hyperpolarising shift the loss-of-function alleles produce.
PMID:16407510 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Thus, diminished function of pacemaker channels is linked to familial bradycardia."
States the gene-disease relationship established by the founding pedigree.
Variants (9)
HCN4 p.Ser672Arg
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Missense change near the cyclic-nucleotide-binding site, identified in a large familial sinus bradycardia pedigree. Mutant channels retain a normal response to cAMP but activate at more negative voltages than wild-type, reducing the inward diastolic current.
Functional effects
hyperpolarizing shift of activation voltage loss-of-function
Activation is displaced to more negative potentials while cAMP responsiveness is retained, so less current flows during diastole at any given membrane potential.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:16407510 SUPPORT In Vitro
"The mutation associated with bradycardia is located near the cAMP-binding site; functional analysis found that mutant channels respond normally to cAMP but are activated at more negative voltages than are wild-type channels."
Characterizes the gating-shift mechanism of this allele.
HCN4 573X (c.1631delC)
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Heterozygous single-base deletion in exon 5 producing a C-terminally truncated subunit that lacks the cyclic-nucleotide-binding domain. Trafficking is normal, but the resulting current is cAMP-insensitive and the subunit exerts a dominant-negative effect on wild-type channels.
Functional effects
loss of cAMP-dependent gating dominant-negative
Truncation removes the cyclic-nucleotide-binding domain, so the channel still reaches the membrane and conducts but no longer responds to cAMP. The subunit also suppresses wild-type current in co-expression.
loss of cAMP-dependent acceleration of the pacemaker current loss-of-function
The direction of effect, recorded alongside the dominant-negative mechanism above: the net result is less cAMP-responsive I_f than a wild-type heterotetramer would carry.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:12750403 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"In a candidate gene approach, a heterozygous 1-bp deletion (1631delC) in exon 5 of the human HCN4 gene was detected in a patient with idiopathic SND."
Identifies the truncating allele in the index patient with idiopathic sinus node dysfunction.
PMID:12750403 SUPPORT In Vitro
"The mutant HCN4 protein (HCN4-573X) had a truncated C-terminus and lacked the cyclic nucleotide-binding domain."
Establishes the structural basis for the loss of cAMP responsiveness.
HCN4 695X
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
C-terminal truncating allele reported in a family with the combined sinus node dysfunction and noncompaction phenotype, in the same series that described G482R and P883R. It is grouped with 573X as a truncation, but unlike 573X it has not been characterised electrophysiologically in the cited work, so its consequence is inferred from the variant class and the family phenotype rather than measured.
Functional effects
reduced pacemaker current loss-of-function
Inferred from the C-terminal truncating class and from the segregating phenotype, not from a functional assay: no patch-clamp characterisation of this allele is presented in the cited report.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:25145518 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Moreover, different truncation (695X) and missense (P883R) HCN4 mutations segregated with a similar combined phenotype in an additional, unrelated family and a single unrelated proband respectively, which both lacked CSRP3-W4R."
Establishes the allele, its truncating class, and its co-segregation with the combined sinus node dysfunction and noncompaction phenotype in an unrelated family.
HCN4 p.Gly480Arg
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Pore-domain missense change segregating with autosomal dominant isolated sinus node dysfunction in a 16-member family. Reduces channel synthesis and membrane trafficking and shifts activation to more negative voltages, yet carries a benign long-term course.
Functional effects
reduced channel synthesis and membrane trafficking loss-of-function
Less channel protein is made and reaches the surface, and what does activate does so at more negative potentials.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:17646576 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Direct sequencing of the exons encoding HCN4 revealed a missense mutation, G480R, in the ion channel pore domain in all affected family members."
Establishes complete co-segregation of the pore-domain allele with the affected status.
HCN4 p.Gly482Arg
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Pore-domain missense change segregating with the combined sinus node dysfunction and biventricular noncompaction phenotype. Mutant subunits are nonfunctional and dominant-negative on wild-type current.
Functional effects
nonfunctional pore with suppression of wild-type current dominant-negative
Homomeric mutant channels carry no current, and the subunit suppresses wild-type current when the two are co-expressed.
reduced pacemaker current loss-of-function
The direction of effect, recorded alongside the dominant-negative mechanism above: nonfunctional subunits assembling into the heterotetramer leave less current than a wild-type channel carries.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:25145518 SUPPORT In Vitro
"HCN4-G482R is located in the highly conserved channel pore domain. Mutant subunits were nonfunctional and exerted dominant-negative effects on wild-type current."
Characterizes the dominant-negative loss-of-function mechanism of this allele.
HCN4 p.Lys530Asn
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
C-linker missense change identified by screening 422 patients with tachyarrhythmias. Homomeric mutant channels behave almost like wild-type; the loss of function appears only in heteromeric mutant/wild-type channels, which show a hyperpolarizing shift of half-maximal activation — the biophysical situation of a heterozygous carrier.
Functional effects
heteromer-restricted hyperpolarizing shift of activation loss-of-function
Homomeric mutant channels are close to wild-type; the shift in half-maximal activation appears only in mutant/wild-type heteromers, which is the configuration a heterozygous carrier actually has.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23178648 SUPPORT In Vitro
"In a candidate gene approach, we screened 422 patients with atrial and/or ventricular tachyarrhythmias and detected a novel HCN4 gene mutation that replaced the positively charged lysine 530 with an asparagine (HCN4-K530N) in a highly conserved region of the C-linker."
Identifies the C-linker allele and the screening context in which it was found.
HCN4 p.Asp553Asn
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Missense change in the C-linker region that reduces the amount of channel reaching the membrane. It is the worked example of the trafficking class of HCN4 allele, and it acts dominant-negatively on the wild-type subunit rather than by simple loss of one copy. The index carrier had recurrent syncope with a prolonged QT interval and torsade de pointes as well as sinus node dysfunction, so this allele's reported phenotype reaches repolarisation and ventricular arrhythmia as well as impulse generation.
Functional effects
trafficking defect reducing surface channel dominant-negative
Less channel reaches the membrane, lowering I_f, and the mutant subunit acts dominant-negatively rather than by simple loss of one copy.
reduced surface pacemaker current loss-of-function
The direction of effect, recorded alongside the dominant-negative mechanism above: less channel at the membrane means less I_f.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:15123648 SUPPORT In Vitro
"In vitro functional study of the D553N mutation showed a reduced membranous expression associated with decreased If currents because of a trafficking defect of the HCN4 channel in a dominant-negative manner."
Establishes both the mechanism class, defective trafficking, and the dominant-negative mode of action of this allele.
PMID:15123648 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A missense mutation, D553N, was found in a patient with sinus node dysfunction who showed recurrent syncope, QT prolongation in electrocardiogram, and polymorphic ventricular tachycardia, torsade de pointes."
Gives the carrier phenotype, which extends beyond the sinus node to repolarization and ventricular arrhythmia.
HCN4 p.Pro883Arg Uncertain Significance
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
C-terminal missense change that does not fit the loss-of-function pattern the rest of these alleles follow. It segregated with the combined sinus node dysfunction and noncompaction phenotype in one proband, but when characterised functionally it shifted activation to more depolarised potentials and, co-expressed with wild-type subunits as in a heterozygote, produced higher rather than lower current density. It is also common enough (minor allele frequency around 0.9%) that it cannot be a fully penetrant cause on its own. The authors of the functional study read it as a modifier acting with a common KCNE1 variant to favour atrial fibrillation and tachycardia-induced cardiomyopathy, rather than as an SSS2 allele. Both readings stand in the literature and neither has been withdrawn.
Functional effects
depolarizing shift of activation with increased current density gain-of-function
The opposite direction from every other allele in this entry. Activation moves to more depolarised potentials and deactivation is faster; in the heterozygous configuration, co-expressed with wild type, current density is higher than wild type rather than lower. This is why the statement that every reported HCN4 disease allele is loss-of-function does not hold for this one.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:25145518 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Moreover, different truncation (695X) and missense (P883R) HCN4 mutations segregated with a similar combined phenotype in an additional, unrelated family and a single unrelated proband respectively, which both lacked CSRP3-W4R."
The observation that puts P883R in this entry at all: it segregated with the combined sinus node dysfunction and noncompaction phenotype in an unrelated proband.
PMID:31481236 REFUTE In Vitro
"Co-transfection of WT and mutant subunits, resembling the heterozygous cellular situation of our patients, revealed significantly higher current densities compared to WT."
Contradicts the loss-of-function reading for this particular allele. Reconstructed in the heterozygous configuration, P883R raises rather than lowers current density, which is the opposite of the mechanism every other allele in this entry works by.
PMID:31481236 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Together with the common KCNE1 variant S38G, previously proposed as a genetic modifier of AF, HCN4-P883R may provide a substrate for the development of AF and TIC."
States the modifier reading the functional study reaches, which is the alternative to treating this as an SSS2 allele.
HCN4 c.1737+1 G>T (splice site)
Gene: HCN4 hgnc:16882 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this variant is in this gene This variant is in HCN4 (hgnc:16882). hgnc:16882 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Canonical splice-donor variant segregating in a large bradycardia family with a two-point LOD score of 4.87. Its interest is that the family was characterised by Holter and exercise testing rather than by patch clamp, so it supplies the quantitative human chronotropic phenotype: carriers reach lower peak heart rates, have a reduced heart rate reserve, and show increased short-term heart rate variability even after normalisation for rate.
Functional effects
reduced pacemaker current loss-of-function
Inferred from the canonical +1 splice-donor class and from the family's bradycardia phenotype rather than measured: this allele was characterised clinically, not by patch clamp.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28465117 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"A novel c.1737+1 G>T 'splice-site' HCN4 mutation was identified in a large family with familial bradycardia which co-segregated with the disease providing a two-point LOD score of 4.87."
Establishes the allele and the strength of its co-segregation with bradycardia in the family.

Medical Actions

5
Permanent Pacemaker Implantation
Action: pacemaker implantationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is pacemaker implantation, annotated with Pacemaker Placement (NCIT:C80434). NCIT:C80434 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Pacemaker Placement NCIT:C80434
Pacing is the definitive therapy for symptomatic bradycardia or symptomatic pauses; it does not correct the channel defect. In HCN4 carriers the age at implantation is later than in SCN5A-related familial sick sinus syndrome, and some HCN4 alleles never require a device. Chronotropic pharmacotherapy may defer but does not replace it.
Mechanism Target:
BYPASSES Sinoatrial Node Automaticity Failure — An implanted pacemaker substitutes an artificial impulse source for the failing sinoatrial node, bypassing rather than repairing the pacemaker current defect.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34383451 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"First-line treatment for patients with confirmed sinus node dysfunction is permanent pacemaker placement with atrial-based pacing and limited ventricular pacing when necessary."
Establishes pacing as the first-line intervention against the failing sinoatrial node.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28104484 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Moreover, HCN4 mutation carriers were more frequently associated with AF (43.8%) and LVNC (50%) and with older age at pacemaker implantation"
Reports age at pacemaker implantation specifically in HCN4 carriers, contrasted with the SCN5A form.
PMID:17646576 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Despite its critical location, this mutation carries a favorable prognosis without the need for pacemaker implantation during long-term follow-up."
Supports the statement in this treatment's description that some HCN4 alleles never require a device: the G480R pore mutation carried a favorable prognosis without pacemaker implantation over long-term follow-up.
Chronotropic Pharmacotherapy (Cilostazol)
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: cilostazol CHEBI:31401 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses cilostazol (CHEBI:31401). CHEBI:31401 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Cilostazol, a phosphodiesterase-3 inhibitor, raises heart rate in sinus node dysfunction and has been used to defer or avoid permanent pacing in symptomatic sick sinus syndrome. It is a rate-raising palliative acting downstream of the pacemaker-current defect rather than a correction of it, and the supporting evidence comes from unselected sick sinus syndrome cohorts, not from genotyped HCN4 carriers.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES Sinoatrial Node Automaticity Failure — Cilostazol raises the sinus rate, partially offsetting the depressed nodal automaticity without restoring the funny current.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:30982679 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"Cilostazol was effective for symptomatic SSS to avoid PM implantation by increasing HR."
Shows a rate-raising effect on the failing sinoatrial node. The cohort is unselected sick sinus syndrome rather than genotyped HCN4 carriers, so the step to SSS2 is an inference from the shared nodal lesion.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:30982679 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"The 6-month PM implantation rate was lower in the cilostazol than the control group (20.4% vs. 55.8%, respectively; p<0.001)."
Quantifies the pacemaker-deferring effect. It is a single-centre retrospective case-control study in unselected sick sinus syndrome, not a randomized trial and not HCN4-genotyped, so the SSS2-specific effect size is unknown.
PMID:33017571 SUPPORT INDIRECT Other
"This review summarizes the current knowledge about SND mechanisms and discusses the possibility of introducing new pharmacologic therapies for treating SND."
Frames pharmacologic management of sinoatrial node dysfunction as an area of active development rather than established practice. It supports the existence of the therapeutic avenue, not the efficacy of any particular agent in SSS2.
Avoidance of Bradycardia-Inducing Medications
Action: supportive careNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is supportive care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Supportive Care NCIT:C15747
Because SSS2 carriers already run a reduced pacemaker current, drugs that further depress sinoatrial automaticity or conduction (beta-blockers, non-dihydropyridine calcium channel blockers, digoxin, and the selective I_f blocker ivabradine, whose molecular target is HCN4 itself) can unmask or worsen symptomatic bradycardia. Extrinsic contributors to sinus node dysfunction are frequently reversible, so medication review is part of management as well as of diagnosis.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34383451 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"The causes of sinus node dysfunction are intrinsic (e.g., degenerative idiopathic fibrosis, cardiac remodeling) or extrinsic (e.g., medications, metabolic abnormalities) to the sinoatrial node."
Establishes medications as an extrinsic cause of sinus node dysfunction, which is the rationale for avoiding rate-slowing drugs in a carrier. The source addresses sinus node dysfunction generally and names no specific agent to avoid in HCN4 carriers, so the carrier-specific application is an inference.
PMID:34383451 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"A diagnosis is made by directly correlating symptoms with a bradyarrhythmia and eliminating potentially reversible extrinsic causes."
Supports elimination of reversible extrinsic causes, of which drug therapy is the main modifiable one. The statement is framed as a diagnostic rather than a therapeutic step, so its use here as a management rationale is an inference.
Stroke Prevention in Atrial Fibrillation
Action: anticoagulation therapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is anticoagulation therapy (NCIT:C63341). NCIT:C63341 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Anticoagulation Therapy NCIT:C63341
Atrial fibrillation occurs in roughly 44% of HCN4 carriers, so thromboembolic prophylaxis is part of routine management once it is documented, prescribed on the same risk-score basis as in any other atrial fibrillation rather than on the genotype. What is genuinely unsettled is the carrier without documented atrial fibrillation: sinus node dysfunction carries an independently raised ischaemic stroke risk even in its absence, and the yearly stroke incidence in sinus node dysfunction with and without atrial fibrillation is indistinguishable, which has been read as evidence of an underlying atrial myopathy rather than of arrhythmia-driven stasis. No trial has tested anticoagulation in that group, and none of this evidence is HCN4-genotyped.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:37977871 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"The average yearly stroke incidence in patients with SND was 1.542% (95% CI: 1.334% to 1.749%). The stroke incidence was similar between the isolated SND (1.587%; 95% CI: 1.510% to 1.664%) and non-isolated (SND+AF) (1.660%; 95% CI: 0.705% to 2.615%) groups."
Quantifies the stroke burden that makes thromboembolic prophylaxis a management question here at all, and shows it does not depend on documented atrial fibrillation. The pooled population is unselected sinus node dysfunction, not HCN4 carriers.
PMID:37977871 NO_EVIDENCE Human Clinical
"Future studies are needed to investigate whether interventions targeting stroke prevention, such as anticoagulation therapy, can help to prevent stroke in patients with SND."
The authors state explicitly that whether anticoagulation prevents stroke in sinus node dysfunction has not been established, so this item is recorded as bearing on the burden rather than on the intervention. It is the reason this treatment is scoped to documented atrial fibrillation.
PMID:40315941 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"In multivariable competing-risk regression modeling accounting for death, isolated SND conferred a significantly increased risk of IS compared to controls (subdistribution hazard ratio 2.28; 95% confidence interval, 1.57-3.31; P < .001)."
A prospective population cohort finding the same excess risk in sinus node dysfunction without atrial fibrillation, which is what makes the untested group a real clinical question rather than a theoretical one. Again the cohort is not genotyped.
Genetic Counseling and Cascade Family Screening
Action: genetic counselingNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is genetic counseling (NCIT:C15240). NCIT:C15240 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Genetic Counseling NCIT:C15240
SSS2 is autosomal dominant with variable penetrance, so first-degree relatives of a proband carry a 50% prior risk and may be affected while entirely asymptomatic. Counselling plus cascade ECG, Holter, echocardiographic and HCN4 sequencing of relatives identifies those carriers, which matters here because the HCN4 phenotype includes a structural (noncompaction) arm that is not detected by rhythm assessment alone.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:17646576 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Sixteen members of a family with sinus bradycardia were evaluated. Evaluation included a clinical questionnaire, 12-lead ECGs, Holter monitoring, echocardiography, and treadmill exercise testing."
Documents the family-wide evaluation protocol by which HCN4 relatives are screened, including the echocardiography needed to catch the structural arm.
PMID:17646576 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Eight family members (5 males) were classified as affected. All affected family members were asymptomatic with normal exercise capacity during long-term follow-up."
Demonstrates the yield of cascade screening in SSS2: half the evaluated family was affected and every affected member was asymptomatic, so relatives would not have been identified without screening.
🔬

Diagnosis

3
Electrocardiography and ambulatory rhythm monitoring
Resting ECG and 24-hour Holter monitoring document the sinus bradycardia, sinus pauses, and bradycardia-tachycardia alternation, and correlate them with symptoms. Exercise testing assesses chronotropic response.
electrocardiography NCIT:C38053 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34383451 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Heart rate monitoring using electrocardiography or ambulatory cardiac event monitoring is performed based on the frequency of symptoms."
Establishes ECG and ambulatory monitoring as the diagnostic modality for sinus node dysfunction.
Cardiac imaging for noncompaction and aortic dimensions
Because the HCN4 form uniquely carries a structural arm, echocardiography and cardiac magnetic resonance are needed to look for left ventricular noncompaction, late gadolinium enhancement, and dilation of the ascending aorta in a carrier who presents only with bradycardia.
echocardiography NCIT:C16525 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:35328031 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"All six patients were diagnosed with LVNC by echocardiography, and five participants additionally by CMR."
Documents echocardiography and cardiac MRI as the modalities that detect the structural arm in HCN4 families.
Genetic testing of HCN4
Targeted or panel sequencing of HCN4 confirms the diagnosis in a proband with familial or early-onset sinus node dysfunction, particularly when bradycardia is accompanied by noncompaction, and enables cascade screening of relatives, many of whom are asymptomatic.
genetic testing NCIT:C15709 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:25145518 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Targeted next-generation and direct sequencing were used for candidate gene analysis and mutation scanning."
Describes the sequencing approach used to establish the HCN4 diagnosis in families with the combined phenotype.
📈

Progression

4
Prenatal and neonatal sinus bradycardia
Age: Second trimester through the neonatal period
The earliest detectable manifestation is a fetal heart rate below the gestational-age norm, which can be accompanied in utero by prominent left ventricular trabeculation. Neither the bradycardia nor the noncompaction is necessarily symptomatic at this stage, and de novo variants occur, so an unremarkable family history does not exclude SSS2 in a bradycardic fetus.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39698436 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Fetal echocardiography revealed a normal fetal heart except for slightly prominent trabeculae in the left ventricle, normal cardiac function, and fetal sinus bradycardia with a heart rate of 100 bpm."
Documents both arms of the SSS2 phenotype, bradycardia and trabecular noncompaction, at the fetal stage in a carrier later shown to have a heterozygous HCN4 in-frame deletion.
Asymptomatic childhood and adolescent bradycardia
Age: Childhood through adolescence
Carriers ascertained through family screening or through an incidental finding are often asymptomatic in this window, with resting bradycardia and noncompaction already present on imaging and no indication for a device. Being asymptomatic is not the same as being low risk: in the same family the adolescent proband was asymptomatic yet had 66 sinus pauses of three seconds or longer, the longest 10.5 seconds, and received a primary-prevention device. This is the phase in which cascade screening is most useful, since the structural findings are established well before symptoms.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35257104 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Her cardiac investigations showed sinus bradycardia, borderline aortic dilatation on echocardiogram and mild biventricular dilatation, NNC, and normal systolic function on cardiac MRI"
A 13-year-old carrier identified by predictive family testing already has bradycardia, noncompaction and borderline aortic dilatation while remaining asymptomatic and device-free.
PMID:35257104 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"His ambulatory electrocardiographic (ECG) monitoring revealed 66 significant asymptomatic sinus pauses (≥3 seconds, the longest lasting 10.5 seconds), mainly nocturnal"
Her 15-year-old brother, in the same family and the same age window, was equally asymptomatic yet carried a heavy pause burden and was implanted, which is why this phase is described as often rather than uniformly device-free.
Symptomatic sinus node dysfunction and device therapy
Age: Late adolescence through mid-adulthood
Clinical manifestation follows adolescence, with a mean age at diagnosis near 40 years and pacemaker implantation on average in the fifth decade. Both are decades earlier than sporadic sick sinus syndrome and roughly two decades later than the SCN5A form, which is what makes age at presentation a usable discriminator between the familial subtypes.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:28104484 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"SSS with HCN4 mutations may form a distinct SSS subgroup characterized by early clinical manifestation after adolescence and frequent association with AF and LVNC."
States the post-adolescent onset window and the accompanying atrial fibrillation and noncompaction burden that define this phase.
PMID:28104484 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Meta-analysis of HCN4 mutation carriers showed a significantly younger age at diagnosis (39.1 ± 21.7 years) than in sporadic SSS (74.3 ± 0.4 years; P <.001), but a significantly older age than in SCN5A mutation carriers (20.0 ± 17.6 years; P = .003)."
Quantifies the age at diagnosis for this phase against both sporadic sick sinus syndrome and the SCN5A form.
Aortic dilatation, onset not reliably age-ordered
Age: Adolescence through adulthood; onset not reliably age-ordered
Ascending aortic and aortic root dilatation has been described as age-dependent in penetrance across HCN4 families, but it has also been found in a 15-year-old and a 13-year-old carrier while absent in their 50-year-old mother, so it is not reliably ordered by age within a family. The practical consequence is that imaging surveillance cannot be deferred in a young carrier on the strength of the age-dependence claim.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35257104 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"20 of 26 patients (77%) and 6/9 (66.6%) patients with the p.Gly482Arg variant had aortic dilatation"
Reports how often aortic dilatation accompanies the HCN4 phenotype. The counts are this paper's summary of an earlier eight-family series rather than its own measurements, so the figure reaches this record through the review step.
PMID:35257104 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"we could not demonstrate the age-dependent penetrance of the aortic dilatation as it was already present in the younger members of this family and absent in the mother"
The same report's own family contradicts a strict age dependence, which is the reason this phase is recorded with a caveat rather than as a staged expectation.
📊

Prevalence

1
First-degree relatives of individuals with pacemaker-treated sinus node dysfunction, Denmark
Lifetime Prevalence 790.0 per 100,000 >1 in 1,000
Cumulative incidence of pacemaker-treated sinus node dysfunction by age 68 was 0.79% in first-degree relatives against 0.27% in the general population, a rate ratio of 2.9 that rose to 5.5 when the index case was implanted at or before 60 years. Study design: nationwide register-based cohort, 6,027,090 individuals followed 1982 to 2022. This is heritable sinus node dysfunction at population scale and is not genotyped, so SSS2 is one of several contributing causes rather than the measured entity, and the recorded rate is the rate for that relative population, not a prevalence for SSS2. It is recorded because it is the only population-scale occurrence figure available for the familial form, and because the inverse relationship with onset age is the epidemiological shadow of exactly the early-onset Mendelian subgroup this entry describes. No general-population prevalence has been reported for SSS2 itself; the HCN4 shares of ascertained cohorts are recorded under genetic.case_fractions, which is the slot for a genetic-spectrum share.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39535890 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"The adjusted rate ratio (RR) of pacemaker-treated SND was 2.9 (2.4-3.6) for individuals having any father, mother, or sibling with a pacemaker implanted on this indication compared with the general population (derived cumulative incidence at the age of 68 years: 0.79 and 0.27%, respectively)."
Gives both the recorded cumulative incidence and its general-population comparator. The cohort is not genotyped, so the step from familial sinus node dysfunction to SSS2 specifically is an inference.
🔀

Differential Diagnoses

5

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Sick Sinus Syndrome 2, Autosomal Dominant:

Familial Sick Sinus Syndrome MONDO:0012061
Overlapping Features The dismech umbrella entry for inherited sinoatrial node dysfunction, which spans several causal genes (HCN4, SCN5A, ANK2, LMNA) and both dominant and recessive transmission. SSS2 is the HCN4-specific, autosomal dominant member of that set and is curated separately because MONDO models it as a distinct disease-series-by-gene entity with its own OMIM record.
Distinguishing Features
  • The umbrella entry is gene-heterogeneous and also spans the autosomal recessive SCN5A form, LMNA and ANK2.
  • SSS2 is defined by a heterozygous HCN4 loss-of-function variant acting on the funny current.
  • Left ventricular noncompaction is a characteristic co-segregating feature of the HCN4 form specifically.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28104484 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Familial sick sinus syndrome (SSS) is often attributable to mutations in genes encoding the cardiac Na channel SCN5A and pacemaker channel HCN4."
States the gene heterogeneity of the umbrella entity, which is what makes the HCN4 form a member of it rather than a synonym for it.
Sick sinus syndrome 1 Not Yet Curated MONDO:0024562
Overlapping Features The SCN5A-related form of familial sick sinus syndrome, in which compound-heterozygous loss-of-function alleles of the cardiac sodium channel reduce myocardial excitability.
Distinguishing Features
  • Recessive rather than dominant transmission.
  • Markedly younger age at diagnosis and at pacemaker implantation than the HCN4 form.
  • Lacks the atrial fibrillation and left ventricular noncompaction burden characteristic of SSS2.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:28104484 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Meta-analysis of HCN4 mutation carriers showed a significantly younger age at diagnosis"
Establishes the age-at-diagnosis separation between HCN4 (SSS2), sporadic sick sinus syndrome, and SCN5A (SSS1) carriers.
Sinoatrial Block MONDO:0020806
Overlapping Features Failure of conduction of the sinoatrial impulse out of the node into atrial myocardium, producing pauses that on the surface ECG can look like sinus arrest.
Distinguishing Features
  • Sinoatrial block is a disorder of impulse exit (conduction); SSS2 is a disorder of impulse generation (automaticity).
  • Sinoatrial exit block may also occur within the SSS2 phenotype, so the distinction is mechanistic rather than mutually exclusive.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34383451 SUPPORT INDIRECT Human Clinical
"Electrocardiography findings include sinus bradycardia, sinus pauses or arrest, sinoatrial exit block, chronotropic incompetence, or alternating bradycardia and tachycardia"
Lists sinoatrial exit block among the electrocardiographic findings of sinus node dysfunction itself, which is why this differential is a mechanistic distinction rather than a mutually exclusive alternative diagnosis.
Sinoatrial Node Dysfunction and Deafness MONDO:0013960
Overlapping Features An autosomal recessive channelopathy caused by biallelic CACNA1D loss-of-function, in which loss of the Cav1.3 L-type calcium current produces both sinoatrial node dysfunction and congenital deafness.
Distinguishing Features
  • Recessive CACNA1D rather than dominant HCN4.
  • The calcium-current lesion is shared with the cochlear inner hair cell ribbon synapse, so congenital sensorineural deafness accompanies the bradycardia.
  • No left ventricular noncompaction.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:21131953 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We describe a human channelopathy (termed SANDD syndrome, sinoatrial node dysfunction and deafness) with a cardiac and auditory phenotype that closely resembles that of Cacna1d(-/-) mice."
Names the entity and its combined cardiac and auditory phenotype, the feature that separates it from SSS2 at the bedside.
PMID:21131953 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We used positional cloning to identify a mutation in CACNA1D, which encodes the pore-forming α1 subunit of Ca(v)1.3 LTCCs, in two consanguineous families with deafness."
Establishes the causal gene and the consanguineous, recessive transmission that contrasts with dominant HCN4.
Sick sinus syndrome 4 Not Yet Curated MONDO:0859173
Overlapping Features Familial sinus node and atrioventricular conduction dysfunction caused by variation in the G-protein subunit gene GNB2.
Distinguishing Features
  • The lesion is in G-protein signalling upstream of the pacemaker channels rather than in the channel itself.
  • Atrioventricular conduction disease is a defining rather than incidental component.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:30645171 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Recently, a gain-of-function mutation in the G-protein gene GNB2 led to enhanced activation of the GIRK (G-protein activated inwardly rectifying K+ channel)."
Places the SSS4 lesion in G-protein signalling acting on a potassium channel, upstream of and distinct from the HCN4 pacemaker channel defect of SSS2. The quoted sentence is this paper's background summarising the primary GNB2 report rather than its own result, which is a KCNJ5 variant in a separate family, so it is cited here for the mechanism class it states and not as the primary source for SSS4.
📊

Related Datasets

3
Single-cell transcriptome analysis reveals CD34 as a marker of human sinoatrial node pacemaker cardiomyocytes [CITE-seq] geo:GSE279632
The two-sample CITE-seq component of a study that resolved the human sinoatrial node into Core Pacemaker, Sinus Venosus and Transitional pacemaker subtypes and identified CD34 as a surface marker enriching functional pacemaker cells. This accession holds only that component; the single-nuclei and single-cell RNA sequencing of fetal and stem cell-derived sinoatrial node cells that the quoted sentence describes sits in sibling accessions of the same series. This is a reference dataset for the cell type whose failure defines SSS2, not an SSS2 patient cohort, and it contains no HCN4 variant carriers. Its value here is that it makes the affected compartment addressable: a CD34 sort yields the pacemaker cells in which an HCN4 allele would have to be assayed.
human SINGLE CELL RNA SEQ
PMID:39604360
Relevance is at the level of cell type rather than genotype. Verified with just verify-datasets.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:39604360 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Here we use single-nuclei/cell RNA sequencing of fetal and human pluripotent stem cell-derived sinoatrial node cells to reveal that they consist of three subtypes of pacemaker cells: Core Pacemaker, Sinus Venosus, and Transitional Cells."
Describes the study this accession belongs to and the material it profiles, human sinoatrial node pacemaker cells at single-cell resolution. The sentence covers the whole series rather than this CITE-seq component alone, which the description states.
A single cell transcriptional roadmap of human pacemaker cell differentiation geo:GSE189782
Single-cell RNA sequencing and trajectory inference over human induced pluripotent stem cells differentiating into sinoatrial-node-like cardiomyocytes, resolving the head, tail and transitional subtypes and the WNT and TGF-beta signalling decisions that separate them. It is the developmental counterpart of the atlas above and the transcriptional map of the platform used by the hiPSC pacemaker-cardiomyocyte model in this entry.
human SINGLE CELL RNA SEQ
PMID:36217819
Wild-type differentiation series, no disease genotypes. Verified with just verify-datasets.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36217819 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Here, we performed single cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) and trajectory inference on human induced pluripotent stem cells (hiPSCs) differentiating to SAN-like cardiomyocytes (SANCMs) to construct a roadmap of transcriptional changes and lineage decisions."
Describes the assay and material behind this accession and ties it to the hiPSC pacemaker-cardiomyocyte platform recorded under experimental_models.
HCN4 gain-of-function mutation increases intrinsic heart rate and limits maladaptive remodeling under pressure overload geo:GSE329403
Cardiac RNA sequencing from HCN4(Y527F) knock-in mice, which carry a gain-of-function C-linker mutation that shifts channel activation to more positive potentials. The allele is the mirror image of the SSS2 lesion, and the mice have a higher intrinsic heart rate with no structural cardiomyopathy up to twelve months. It is included here as the opposite-direction control on the same gene: it is the closest available transcriptional readout of altered HCN4 channel availability in myocardium, and it bears on whether the structural arm of SSS2 can be a consequence of the rate change alone.
house mouse BULK RNA SEQ
PMID:42358349
Triage note: this is a gain-of-function allele in mouse, not the human loss-of-function lesion of SSS2, and it must not be read as an SSS2 expression dataset. Verified with just verify-datasets.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42358349 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We generated HCN4(Y527F) knock-in mice (HCN4F) carrying a GOF mutation in the C-linker of HCN4 channels, which shifts their activation curves to more positive potentials."
Describes the genotype behind this accession and makes explicit that the direction of effect is opposite to the SSS2 lesion.

Experimental Models

2
Patient-derived hiPSC lines carrying a heterozygous and a homozygous HCN4 variant (UKMi009-A, UKMi011-A) IPSC_DERIVED_MODEL
Two human induced pluripotent stem cell lines reprogrammed from members of a consanguineous family with sinus node dysfunction, one carrying the familial HCN4 variant heterozygously and the other homozygously. The pair is a gene-dose series in the patients' own genetic background and at the native locus, which is the closest available human substrate for the SSS2 lesion. As published, the lines are characterised only for pluripotency (karyotype, marker expression, three-germ-layer differentiation); no cardiomyocyte electrophysiology is reported, so nothing yet establishes that they reproduce the funny-current deficit.
Organism
human NCBITaxon:9606 NCBI Taxonomy (NCBITaxon) Relation: this experimental model is built in this organism This experimental model is built in human, annotated with Homo sapiens (NCBITaxon:9606). NCBITaxon:9606 is an organism from the NCBI Taxonomy.
Cell source
Reprogrammed somatic cells from two members of a consanguineous family with inherited sinus node dysfunction.
Publication
PMID:40414080
Pathograph links
HCN4 Loss-of-Function Variant perturbs fidelity: unknown The lines carry the family's own HCN4 variant at the endogenous locus in one and two copies, instantiating the genetic lesion of this node as a dose series rather than as an overexpressed construct.
Limitations: The family is consanguineous and the report does not say which members are affected, so it is not established that the heterozygous carrier has the disease; in a consanguineous pedigree segregating a homozygous allele the heterozygote may well be an unaffected carrier. Neither line is therefore known to instantiate the dominant heterozygous genotype of SSS2, and the homozygous state has no counterpart in it at all. The report also does not characterise the variant as loss-of-function: the link to this node rests on the gene, not on a demonstrated functional consequence. The publication reports pluripotency characterisation alone, with no cardiomyocyte differentiation, no I_f recording and no beating rate, so the functional fidelity of the model is untested rather than moderate or high.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:40414080 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Here, we generated two hiPSC lines from a consanguineous family with SND where the HCN4 variant was either present in heterozygous or homozygous state."
Establishes that the lines carry the patient HCN4 variant in a gene-dose series, which is the perturbation this link records.
